# Decisions — one line each, newest first
Owner: the Lead records; the founder decides product choices. Only decisions that change what is built or how we work. Weighty ones get a page in `decisions/`. Superseded lines are struck through, never deleted; when this file passes 100 lines, superseded lines move to `history/`.

| # | Date | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| P-823 | 25 Nov | Kind: workflow. **Additive migrations, money and public-route changes are labelled automatically after the fresh Opus reviewer's Approve on the exact head and green ci; the Lead reads afterwards: every money PR within the hour, others 1 in 3 (founder, 25 Nov ~08:1x IST, typed turn to Lead 3, his choice of the recommended option; A380).** Workflows, permissions and destructive migrations (drop, rename, delete) still wait for the Lead's read first. Relaxes P-423 and P-731 for those kinds only. Evidence: 209 queue-gate holds for a Lead read in 72 h (`design/why-targets-missed-2026-11-24.md`); #3490 waited 4 h on 24-25 Nov. Built by OS-2 in the queue gate. Impact: time removes most Lead-read waits; cost none; quality kept by the after-read and the reviewer. |
| P-822 | 25 Nov | Kind: workflow. **`pr-suites` (the PR's affected suites) becomes required before the label (founder, 25 Nov ~08:1x IST, typed turn to Lead 3, his choice of the recommended option; A380).** Partly reverses P-651 (2) (set for CI capacity before the move to Enterprise Cloud; job wait is now median 0 min). Evidence: a third of merge batches fail; 18 of 33 real defects in 72 h were already red in pr-suites. The Lead reports batch failures for 24 h after it lands. Built by OS-2. | P-803 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **The Lead never acts on stale information (founder, typed 14:5x IST 22 Nov to Lead 4: "make sure inbuilt a enforced mechanism in the lead agent that he doesn't take any stale information. Always verify the source of information and whether the information is up to date … Never assumes it").** Every fact the Lead reports or acts on is read fresh from its source in that turn (origin/main after a fetch, the PR or run by API, the clock with `TZ=Asia/Kolkata date`) and carries its source and time; local branch copies, old reports, cached script output and memory are leads to check, never evidence. Incidents that set it (22 Nov): a leftover temporary git index made commit 54e27fc66 delete 10,753 files on the Lead branch (restored by 7ea0d81f3 at 14:59 IST); times guessed as 15:1x when the clock said 14:4x; lanes started from 18 Nov qa notes for parts merged since. Enforcers: lane "Enforcement 2 · no stale information, no bulk deletes" (ENF-803: bulk-delete and temp-index guards, freshness stamps in lead-watch, `fresh-read.sh`, brief-check needs a main sha and time). |
| P-802 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **Tracks stop waiting on each other in the merge queue and in tests; quality unchanged (founder, typed 14:4x-14:5x IST 22 Nov to Lead 4: "separate queues for track A, B, C … all the best in class solution you said … strongly enforced"; "do only things which improves the quality and speed").** (1) Mergify partitions per track (Track A, Track B, records; a PR on both tracks goes to a shared partition), batch size 3 each; (2) qa proof runs the row's journey on the merge batch's tree; (3) migrations re-stamped at queue time, one slot per Prisma schema area instead of one product-wide; (4) PRs run only affected suites, while the merge batch and nightly run everything. Built and enforced in GitHub config and CI by lanes "Speed 1" (1, 2) and "Speed 2" (3, 4), started 14:59 IST; live when each PR merges. Until then the Lead applies them by hand (P-791 still: no hand merges while batches run). |
| P-801 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **Domain, CRM and technical choices come from sources, never from a founder question (founder, typed 14:4x and 14:4x IST 22 Nov to Lead 4: "Don't ask me question related to domain because it's a government domain, and you have to find what are the best practices in the industry, competitive analysis, government RCIC documents"; "every generic stuff … should come from the market research and the RCSCI documents").** Order of sources: the team's requirements (AGENTS rule 2), then official government pages (IRCC, ESDC, CRA, CICC for RCICs) cited with page, quote and date, then industry practice and competitors (market research), then technical standards; the Lead or Lead B decides and records it with the citation. Only a product preference with no source goes to the founder, with a "Sources checked:" line and the recommended option first. Enforcer owed (Enforcement lane, 14:4x): `brief-check.sh` refuses a brief or founder question that states a domain value or asks a domain question without a `Source:` / `Sources checked:` line. |
| P-800 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **Plan two to three days of capability ahead on every track, so the pipeline never drains or floods (founder, typed 14:4x IST 22 Nov to Lead 4: "We plan for 2, 3 day capability so that my pipeline doesn't get drained completely or queue or overflowed completely … constant smooth flow"; root cause: Track B ran 4 builders on deep rows and 7 partly built capabilities had no track).** Each track board (`design/board-lead-a-*.md`, `design/board-lead-b-*.md`) lists the next 2-3 days of capabilities, nearest to done first, each open part with a lane or a ready brief; steps 1, 2 and 4 (build, review, journey written) run without limit because each lane has its own machine; step 3 (merge queue, CI machines, the one migration slot) is paced so the queue holds about one batch ahead; every partly built capability belongs to a track. Enforcer owed (Enforcement lane, 14:4x): lead-watch ACT when a track's ready buffer is under 2 days, an open part on a board has no lane, or a Partial capability is on no board. |
| P-798 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **The Lead's triggers follow the 10-minute check (Lead 4, 14:4x IST 22 Nov; founder, typed 14:4x: "are these optimized figures or they are just taken blindly?").** The 30/60/90-minute thresholds came from the 30-minute check of early November, not from data; today they fired late (questions waited 53-158 min, an approved PR 149 min outside the queue). | P-797 | 22 Nov | Kind: scope. **Whose province sets the tax on a client invoice (R1.56): the client's (Lead 4, 14:4x IST 22 Nov, from official sources; the founder, typed 14:4x: domain questions are settled from government sources and industry practice, not asked).** CRA GST/HST Memorandum 3-3-6 "Place of Supply in a Province - General Rules for Services" (canada.ca, last updated 2026-04-09), para 20: "a supply of a service is made in a province if, in the ordinary course of business of the supplier, the supplier obtains one of the following addresses"; its Example 6 (an Ontario consulting firm serving a Nova Scotia company): "The supply of the service is therefore made in Nova Scotia". So: each invoice carries an editable "Bill-to province", filled from the client's Canadian address on the record; with no Canadian address, General Rule 2 (where the service is performed) applies, which for our firms is the firm's province. A client outside Canada: zero-rating as an exported service is likely but not confirmed from canada.ca (Memorandum 3-3, Schedule VI Part V not read): labelled Sample, tax shown as the firm's province until confirmed. Supersedes Lead B's interim "firm's province" in FO-7g part 1. Tax readings: Unconfirmed: no accountant. |

| P-791 | 22 Nov | Kind: workflow. **The unclog playbook is a rule, with today's lesson, and gets code enforcers (founder, typed 12:4x IST 22 Nov to Lead 2): "many time you have managed to do many things, like … identifying the bad PR in the entire batch … and manually merging, testing and fixing it … have we enforced this as a code or a decision so that it gets always in agents learning curve? … Is there any opportunity to manually unblock and help unclog, drain, fast track?"** (1) **Find the bad PR:** Mergify's parallel mode already tests a failed batch's PRs alone; the Lead reads its summary ("failed tested alone on top of main" = the PR's own fault: owner fixes with a test; otherwise re-run the red part alone, P-371) and never relabels without a green head (P-731). (2) **Manual merge only when it does not reset running batches:** any push to main outside the queue restarts every batch being tested. Lesson 22 Nov: Lead 2's by-hand merge of #2754 at 12:1x IST restarted 9 batches, about 30 minutes of merges lost. So the Lead merges by hand (P-757) only when the queue has no batch running, or when the merge itself unblocks every running batch (a red check on main). Otherwise the fix goes through the queue. (3) **Drain levers, in order:** a) keep failing PRs out (label only on a green head); b) proof-unlocking PRs ("Completes: R1.n") first, via a Mergify priority rule applied in a quiet window; c) more PRs per batch (2 → 3 or 4) when machines are the wait and batch failures stay under 10%; d) more machines (AWS-hosted runners) priced first and only when c) is not enough; e) never cancel a CI run. (4) **Enforced, owed to the enforcement lane:** `lead-watch.sh` prints the number of batches running and says "a manual merge now restarts N batches"; a `tools/lead-merge.sh` the Lead uses for by-hand merges refuses while batches run unless given a reason line; the Mergify priority rule for "Completes:" PRs. Until live, the Lead checks the batch count by hand before any by-hand merge. Impact (P-254): time: avoids ~30-minute resets; cost none; quality same gates. |

It holds the commercial and pricing documents (`commercial/`, `ai-workers/commercial/`) and the name-free copy of the team's requirements (`domain/requirements-*`); removing them would not remove them from history, and history is never rewritten (AGENTS rule 1). Going private means paid test minutes (about US$100–130 a day at today's pace, *estimate*) or the slower Mac runner. Choices offered: private with the Mac runner, private with paid minutes, or public with sensitive material kept out from now on; the founder kept it as is for now. Revisit when the founder asks. Confirms P-224 (3). |

The tech lead checks each branch on GitHub, then closes the lane, merges all records once, archives the Mac sessions and removes the evidence worktree (A29, A30). Approved PRs keep merging through Mergify after the sessions stop. Why: no half-done rows and nothing redone by the next sessions; the unused plan of this account costs nothing extra. |

Rules: (1) every builder and qa session holds a written queue of at least two ready rows in its brief, and after labelling a PR it starts the next row at once without waiting for the tech lead (the WIP limit P-231 (3) still applies: one PR in build, one in review); when its queue is empty it comments "queue empty" on its last PR; (2) every check of the tech lead opens with the session list, and any session idle with work left gets its next step in that same check; (3) the daily measures add "builder idle minutes with work left". |

Seven steps built once and used by every library: search first; a fixed rules check (type, source and reason, no duplicate, references intact, no rule conflict, redundancy, gap or loop); the AI co-pilot drafts a change set that passes the same rules and never writes directly; an impact preview of everything it touches; the person confirms; review by risk (founder: "By risk (Recommended)": wording, labels and aliases direct and logged; new items, meaning changes, rule changes and anything in a released combination need a second person, the expert where P-96 says); a versioned release marked made by person or AI, with roll-back. (4) The co-pilot drafts the whole item: name, type, format, scope, source, where used, and why no existing item fitted. (1) The engine, contracts and tests are made lift-ready to ERv2's rules. Engine: resolver, rules and gates, field maps, PDF writer with read-back, answer-key runner, AI drafting and checker. (2) Screens, API and database are a fully tested reference the team rebuilds on ERv2. (3) The hand-over is a package the team pulls; we never push to the team's repositories (AGENTS rule 1); who does the final port is for the founder to agree with the team's tech lead. A library retire (field, section, document type, form, check) takes effect at approval; a structure name or combination at the next release; open cases and earlier releases keep what they used.
